  - Correctness rests on never missing a change, so both ways one can go missing are handled and tested: a frame dropped under back-pressure, and a frame replaced before it was ever rendered. `NotBeingToldAboutADroppedFrameIsWhatWouldLoseIt` fails when the notification is removed, so that call is known to be load-bearing rather than assumed to be. A change of covered rectangle forces a full repaint, because a patch computed against different framing is nonsense.
  - Still open: **compositing itself is CPU raster**, which is the other half and the larger one at 4K. Left deliberately — moving it needs a decision, and the brush engine specifically must stay on the CPU (`RuntimeDeterminismTests` pins a bit-identical fingerprint, and GPU rasterisation is not bit-identical across drivers, so a GPU stamp would break reload, undo re-render and inbetweening). Readback is the other trap: `GetPixel`/`ReadPixels` appear in `BrushEngine`, `FloodFill`, `SymbolRasterizer`, `TextureRegistry` and `TiledRasterizer`, and each would become a pipeline stall against a GPU-resident canvas.
  - The owner's own hypothesis, and it is correct: *"perhaps we render too much with the CPU instead of the GPU even though the info bar states GPU."*
  - **Every compositing surface in the pipeline is CPU raster.** `SKSurface.Create(SKImageInfo)` with no `GRContext` is a raster surface by the Skia contract, and that is the overload used at `ComposeRing.cs:94`, `SceneRenderer.cs:72`, `MainViewModel.cs:10107` (culled) and `:9955` (unbounded); `FrameRasterizer.cs:28` is a CPU `SKBitmap` plus a CPU `SKCanvas`. Nothing in the solution ever creates a `GRContext` — it only receives whatever lease Avalonia's compositor hands it.
  - **The status bar says "GPU" and means something else.** `CanvasControl.GraphicsBackend` reports whether Avalonia has a `GrContext` for blitting the finished frame to the window. It says nothing about where layer compositing happened. It misled the owner exactly as written, which makes the wording part of the defect rather than incidental to it.
  - `GuidePainter.PaintDocument` draws the **whole** artwork image every publish regardless of how small the dirty region was, and every publish hands over a brand-new `SKImage` from `Surface.Snapshot()` — so a GPU-backed lease cannot recognise it as a partially-changed texture and must upload all of it: **8.3 MB per publish at 1080p, 33.2 MB at 4K** (computed from the surface geometry, not timed — this container has no GPU context, so the upload latency itself is unmeasured here and that is stated rather than estimated away).
  - This is the difference the owner is feeling against Krita, which keeps per-tile GPU textures and re-uploads only the dirty tiles. Fixing it is a compositor change rather than a patch — it wants a design note and an owner decision, not an afternoon. Cost: L
  - P2 rather than P1 because B121 removes the acute regression and painting is proportional to the dab again; this is the ceiling above it, not a break.

- [x] **B112** `P2` `canvas` Anchors and collision boxes did not move on screen until the drag was released `evidence: RigPreviewTests, AnchorsFollowThePointerWhileTheGroupIsBeingDragged, CollisionBoxesFollowThePointerWhileTheGroupIsBeingDragged, AGroupPreviewIsMeasuredFromWhereTheDragWasPickedUp, AnchorsAndBoxesMeasureFromTheirOwnPress, ADraggedCornerPreviewsAResizeRatherThanAMove, ThePreviewLeavesTheMarksItWasGivenAlone, ClearingThePreviewGoesBackToWhatTheRecordSays`
  - Repro: select two anchors and drag. Nothing moves until the pointer is released, then they jump.
  - **Filed with the wrong cause, and worth recording why.** It was written up as the fourth instance of B109's pattern — a group path that should move live on the record like the guides and reference boxes do — and it is not. `DragRig` writes on release *by design*, and says so: "one editor step per call rather than one per gesture, which is the simple reading and the wrong one for a long drag", and "live feedback during the drag is the overlay's own business and touches nothing". For rig marks the record is deliberately left alone until the release.
  - The actual cause is that the overlay's half was never built. `RigOverlayPainter.Paint` renders straight from `RigMarks`, which reads the document, so there was nothing to show a drag with. That makes this **not** a Phase 6 regression: the single-mark drag had never previewed either, from B58 onwards.
  - Fix: a `_rigPreview` on `CanvasControl` — chrome, not data, the same bargain `DraftGuide` makes — applied at the paint site by `WithRigPreview`. One mechanism covers every rig drag, because `RigOverlay.Drag` already decides what a delta means: a corner resizes, `RigCorner.None` translates, so the preview is the arithmetic the commit will use rather than a second guess at it. Measured from the press rather than summed from the increments, for the reason B109 paid for. Cost: S
  - The lesson is the one `docs/DESIGN-performance.md` records for measurement, pointed at diagnosis: the symptom was real and the attribution was not. Three sibling bugs had the same cause, so the fourth was assumed to share it, and the comment on the path said otherwise.

- [x] **B82** `P2` `canvas` The compositor cannot know what is on screen, so nothing can be culled to the viewport `evidence: SetViewport, ACulledComposePutsTheArtWhereAFullComposeDoes, ViewportCullingPerformanceTests, AWholeCanvasPublishStillComposesOnlyTheViewport, APointerEventInACulledViewportRepaintsTheDabNotTheViewport`
  - Repro: read `PublishSnapshot`. `viewWidth`/`viewHeight` are `scene.Width`/`scene.Height` whenever `cameraView` is null — which is the ordinary case and every asset document — so the compose surface is the **whole document** at `ComposeScale`, and `CanvasControl` pans and zooms the finished snapshot afterwards.
  - The view model is told a scalar and nothing else. `SetDisplayScale(double)` is the only channel, and `_displayScale` feeds `ComposeScale`. Zoom and pan live in `CanvasControl` (`_zoom`), downstream of the composite, so at the moment the compositing happens nothing in the process knows which document rectangle is visible.
  - Cause: not an oversight, a boundary. Invariant 5 says the view transform is view-only, and keeping it in the view is what has made that true. Compositing the whole document and letting the canvas frame the result is the straightforward reading of that rule — it is correct, and it is what makes the viewport unavailable.
  - `docs/DESIGN-infinite-canvas.md` claimed the visible rectangle was "already computable" from `CameraTransform.DeviceBounds`, and that was a conflation: `CameraTransform` maps under a `CameraFraming`, the *authored* camera, which is a document property rather than the view. The document now carries the correction inline rather than the claim.
  - **This is the blocker under three other entries, which is why it is filed on its own rather than inside any of them.** B29 cannot use a region for a frame change, B30's whole-frame rebuild can only "stop being paid" once a miss rebuilds the visible tiles alone, and an unbounded canvas has no `scene.Width` to compose in the first place. `TileCompositor` and `RecompositingCostsWhatIsOnScreenNotWhatExists` already hold the culling property; there is simply nothing to cull against.
  - Fix: report the visible document rectangle from the canvas the way `SetDisplayScale` already reports scale, compose that rectangle rather than the document, and let `CanvasControl` draw a viewport-sized snapshot. It stays inside invariant 5 — a rectangle the view offers as *input to a cache decision* mutates no stroke — but it changes what a `RenderSnapshot` means, makes `ComposeRing`'s stale regions viewport-relative, and touches the second-riskiest file in the repository alongside the canvas control. Wants its own design note rather than an afternoon. Cost: L
  - P2 because it blocks work rather than corrupting art, and because it is load-bearing for more than it looks: two open performance bugs and the whole point of the infinite canvas are all waiting on this one change.
  - **Landed, and the line it turns on is the one that mattered.** `ComposeViewportCulled` composes the clamped visible rectangle; the snapshot carries the rectangle its image covers, and `GuidePainter.PaintDocument` draws it there. Measured on a 960×540 document culled to `{200,120,760,420}`: the surface falls from 960×540 to 560×300 and **0 pixels** of the art move (`ACulledComposePutsTheArtWhereAFullComposeDoes`, with the contact sheet beside it). Two conditions the first attempt did not have, both of which broke it — the rectangle is **clamped to the document**, because a zoomed-out view reports `{-480,-450,1921,1440}` for a 960×540 canvas and an unclamped rectangle is a source rect off the end of the bitmap; and culling only engages when the clamped rectangle is **actually smaller**, so the ordinary fitted view keeps the `ComposeRing` path and its dirty-region optimisation.
  - **It also caused B120, which is the part worth reading before touching this again.** Culling is a rendering decision, and the pointer mapping is not allowed to know it happened.
  - **Closed 2026-08-08 by renaming the evidence to the tests that shipped, not by writing new ones.** The entry promised `ViewportCullingTests` and `ARepaintCompositesOnlyTheVisibleRegion`; what landed is `ViewportCullingPerformanceTests`, whose `AWholeCanvasPublishStillComposesOnlyTheViewport` asserts exactly that property (image smaller than the document, covering the clamped window), with the B121 regression and the stale-ring hazard beside it and the zero-drift pixel proof in `CursorAlignmentTests`. The mechanism, the tests and the scars were all in the tree; only the names in this line were fiction.

- [x] **B59** `P2` `canvas` An effect brush rendered through tiles cannot read across a tile boundary `evidence: AnEffectBrushAcrossATileBoundaryIsMeasuredRatherThanAssumed`
  - Found while building the tiled render, by the test written to prove it identical rather than by a report — which is the point of `ATiledRenderIsBitIdenticalToAnUntiledOne` existing before the render path moves.
  - Repro: a 40 px Smudge dragged from (200,200) to (320,220) across the seam at x = 256, on a 256 px grid. Measured **3876 pixels differ from the untiled render, worst alpha delta 191** out of 255. Paint strokes are bit-identical at tile sizes 64, 100, 256 and 512, including every dab dynamic seeded from position — so this is not the tiling arithmetic.
  - Cause: `TiledRasterizer` passes the tile as `targetPixels`, and that is what an effect brush *reads*. A tile only holds its own pixels, so a dab near the edge samples transparent black where the untiled render sees the neighbouring tile's paint.
  - **The damage is not confined to the seam, and that is worth writing down because the first hypothesis said it would be.** Only 62% of the differing pixels were within a brush width of the boundary. A smudge is a *stateful walk* — `carried = Mix(sample, carried, carryOver)` — so one wrong sample at the seam is inherited by every later dab, and the error travels the rest of the stroke. The test now asserts the true containment instead: **100% of the differences fall inside the smudge's own footprint**, which is what a read-neighbourhood defect can touch and nothing wider.
  - **Fixed, and not the way this entry predicted.** Effect brushes are refused by the tiled path (`TiledRasterizer.CanTile`) and their frames render whole and are sliced into the store, so the pixels come from the untiled path and cannot disagree with it. Measured: **0 differing pixels**, and the store still holds **1 tile of the 12 the document spans**, so what stays resident is proportional to ink even on the fallback. The cost is one document-sized allocation *while the frame renders* — today's peak memory, kept only for frames that actually contain an effect brush.
  - **The padded-read fix below was tried and is wrong, which is worth keeping rather than deleting.** Rendering the neighbourhood into an offset scratch made it *worse*: 3265 wrong pixels against 3876, with the worst alpha delta rising from 191 to a full **255**. The reason is the real root cause, and it is deeper than "a tile is too small to read": `SampleAverage` indexes its read bitmap with the dab's **document** position — `Math.Clamp((int)(pos.X + dx * spread), 0, pixels.Width - 1)` — and `LerpDab` does the same. The engine assumes **the read bitmap's origin is the document's origin**, which was true of every caller that existed before tiling. A shifted scratch violates that, and a sample read from the wrong place is further from the truth than one clamped short.
  - **Left open as B60**: thread a read origin through the effect path so the engine stops assuming, which is what lets an effect brush tile properly instead of opting out. Not done here on purpose — it edits the hottest and most invariant-critical file in the repository, and putting the discovery and the surgery in one diff is what the branch conventions exist to stop. `EffectBrushesAreRefusedByTheTiledPathUntilB59IsFixedProperly` pins the refusal so the fallback cannot be removed silently, and it is written to fail the day B60 lands.
  - Superseded plan, kept for the reasoning: render an effect brush against a **padded read source** — the tile plus a margin of its neighbours, at least the dab radius and the smudge sampling offset. That is a real cost (the margin is re-rasterised per tile) and it only applies to `BrushKind.Smudge`/`Blur`, so the paint path keeps the cheap read. The alternative — flatten the neighbourhood on demand — trades memory for the same thing.
  - **P2 rather than P1 because nothing renders through tiles yet.** `TiledRasterizer` is not wired into the canvas, the cache or export, so no artist can reach this today. It becomes a P1 the moment the render path moves, and it is the reason that move must not happen before this is fixed. Cost: M

- [x] **B21** `P2` `canvas` Shrink then Grow walks a selection into its top-left corner `evidence: ShrinkAndGrowLeaveTheSelectionWhereItWas, ACircleShrinksByTheSameAmountOnEverySide`
  - Repro: draw a circular marquee, then Shrink and Grow a few times. The top and left creep in about two pixels a cycle; the right and bottom do not move. Reported as "circle shrinks from the top left".
  - Cause: `TraceBoundary` walks pixel centres, so the polygon it returns runs down the middle of the boundary ring rather than around the outside of it. Filling it back keeps only the pixels whose centres are strictly inside, and Skia resolves the exact-half case towards the bottom right — so the top and left rings are lost on every round trip and the bottom and right survive.
  - Fix: when the contours being rasterised came from a trace, stroke the path as well as filling it, which puts the ring back and makes the round trip stable. Scoped to the selection adjust, because a contour drawn by hand is a geometric outline and filling it is already right. The tracer itself still reports centres — the honest fix there is a corner-lattice trace, and it is shared with every flood fill, so it is not something to change inside an unrelated commit. Cost: M

- [x] **B20** `P2` `canvas` Shrink does nothing to a selection touching the canvas edge `evidence: ShrinkingTheWholeCanvasPullsInFromAllFourEdges, AnEdgeTouchingSelectionShrinksOnTheEdgeItTouches`
  - Repro: magic-wand the whole document, or drag a marquee off the left edge, then Shrink. Select All then Shrink moved the selection one pixel and no more; a marquee touching one edge shrank on three sides.
  - Cause: erosion is a dilation of the complement, and `Dilate1` treated everything beyond the bitmap as empty. For a full-canvas selection the complement is empty, so there was nothing anywhere to grow inward from. The one pixel that did move was the round-trip artifact of B21, not erosion.
  - Fix: the complement includes what is off the edge. `Dilate` takes what lies outside, false for a real dilate and true for the inverted pass inside `Erode`. Cost: S

- [x] **B17** `P2` `canvas` Guides are invisible over the drawing `evidence: GuidePainter, GuidePainterTests, AGuideIsVisibleOverAnOpaqueDrawing, TheArtStillReadsThroughIt`
  - Repro: place any guide on a new document. It shows on the grey surround and vanishes the moment it crosses the canvas.
  - Cause: mine, and the comment I wrote made it sound deliberate. `DrawGuides` ran before the artwork on the reasoning that "a ruler on paper is something you draw over" — but a new document opens with an opaque background layer, so under the drawing means under a sheet of white. The analogy does not survive an opaque bottom layer.
  - Fix: draw guides over the artwork, translucent. The thing the old order was protecting — not hiding the drawing — is paid for with alpha instead.
  - Was `evidence: manual`, and no longer is. The obstacle was real — the rig is painted inside a Skia lease the headless platform never grants — but the answer was to move the painting somewhere a test can call rather than to give up: `GuidePainter` is pure Skia and takes a canvas, and `PaintDocument` owns the checkerboard/artwork/guides order, because splitting those three apart is exactly how the bug happened. Putting the guides back underneath fails five of the seven tests. Cost: S

- [x] **B184** `P3` `canvas` The render report counts composites made while GPU compositing was *off* as fallbacks, so a bisect capture reads as a failure `evidence: GpuCompositeToggleTests, SwitchingItOnForgetsWhatHappenedWhileItWasOff`
  - Repro, and it is the one that happened: play a scene with GPU compositing off, write a report, tick the toggle on, play again, write a second report. The second reads `of the publishes that could use the card: 160 did, 207 fell back`. Nothing fell back. The 207 are the first playback.
  - `GpuComposite.GpuComposites` and `CpuComposites` are process-lifetime tallies and **nothing in the application ever calls `ResetCounters`** — only tests do. `CreateSurface` increments one of the two on every call whatever the toggle says, so composites made while the path was off accumulate in the CPU tally and are then printed under a heading that presumes the card was available.
  - **P3 by blast radius and P1 by the company it keeps.** It changes no pixel and no artist will ever see the line. It appeared inside B179 — a live P1 memory investigation whose whole method is comparing two captures either side of that exact toggle — and produced a false signal in the direction of "GPU allocation is now failing under memory pressure", which is a plausible enough story to have been chased.
  - The report already distinguishes a real refusal: `RefusedAllocations` and `RefusedTooLarge` each print a `!!` line, and **neither appeared**. That is what caught it. A reader who has not memorised `AppendCompositor` has no such tell, which is the second half of the fix below.
  - **Fixed by clearing the tallies when the toggle moves**, in the `SettingEnabled` setter rather than at the call sites, so an environment-variable run and a Configure-window tick get the same behaviour. Nothing is lost: the report only ever prints the counts for the mode that is currently running, so the count of the mode you have left is unreachable by construction.
  - **And the word "fell back" now says which kind it was.** With both refusal tallies at zero the line states that the card was never asked — those publishes drew on a lease with no graphics context — because a fallback and a refusal are different events and only one of them is a fault. This is the fourth false line this file has had, and the previous three are recorded in `AppendCompositor`'s own doc comment for the same reason.

- [x] **B173** `P3` `canvas` Delete and Backspace do nothing to a marquee selection `evidence: ToolSelectionFillTests, DeleteClearsWhatIsInsideTheMarquee, BackspaceFillsTheMarqueeWithTheBackgroundColour`
  - Reported as a missing behaviour: Backspace should fill the selection with the background colour, Delete should clear what is inside it. Both are what every raster application does, and neither is bound.
  - Delete on the canvas is already `lines.delete` — it removes selected vector lines — and Backspace is unbound outside the Layers panel, where it is `docker.clearLayer`.
  - **The precedence was decided rather than guessed: a live marquee wins, and lines are the fallback.** That is Photoshop's rule and the one an artist arrives with. It is recorded here with what it costs, because it goes against the codebase's existing precedent: `NudgeSelection` asks the line selection *first* and falls through to the marquee, and it says why in a comment. So after this lands the two keys disagree about which selection they mean, and Delete silently changes meaning for anyone working with the Arrow while a stale marquee is still up — which is exactly the case B171 makes more likely than it should be. A fix should land B171 first, or the new rule will fire on selections the artist thought were gone.
  - The fill is a `ToolKind.Fill` stroke with the selection's contours, and the clear an eraser stroke with the same — both go through the record, so invariant 3 holds and undo is free.
  - P3 on annoyance × common. Cost: S.
  - **Fixed, and the sentence above about the clear was wrong in a way the test caught immediately.** "An eraser stroke with the same contours" does not erase a region: an eraser's `Points` are a *path* the engine walks laying dabs, and a fill's are a *contour* it fills — so the first version laid a stroke down the outline of the marquee and left the middle untouched. The test asserted alpha 0 inside the region and read 255, which is the number that made the difference visible rather than arguable.
  - **So the record gained `ToolKind.ClearRegion`**: same contour convention as `Fill`, composited `DstOut` instead of over. Its own kind rather than a closed eraser stroke, because nothing about the geometry says which reading is meant — a renderer asked to guess would guess wrong on any short eraser stroke, and that failure would be silent and rare, the worst shape a rendering bug can have. Enums serialize as camelCase strings (`DocJson`), so inserting a member does not shift anything an existing document holds.
  - `StampFill` now serves both and one line separates them; everything else — contour, clip, anti-aliasing, opacity — is identical, which is the argument for putting them through one path.
  - **The key move cost two tests, and they were updated rather than worked around.** Delete came off `lines.delete` and went to a new `select.clear`, because two registrations on one gesture is the conflict `ShortcutMapTests` refuses by design. `lines.delete` keeps its id — so an artist's rebinding survives — and loses its default, and the fallback inside the command means Delete still deletes lines when no region is up. `ContextShortcutTests` and `StrokeActionTests` pinned the old arrangement and now pin the new one, with the reason written where the assertion is.
  - **B171 landed first, as this entry asked.** The disagreement with `NudgeSelection` remains and remains knowing.

- [x] **B172** `P3` `canvas` The white arrow cannot select or enter anything `evidence: PathEditingTests, TheWhiteArrowEntersAPathByClickingIt, TheWhiteArrowPicksAPointWithoutLosingTheOthers`
  - Repro: pick Direct select, click a drawn line. Nothing happens — no points, no handles, no selection.
  - `CanvasControl`'s `CanvasToolMode.PathEdit` case does one thing: `_grabPathPart`, which grabs a node or handle **of a path that is already in isolation**. The call that *puts* a path into isolation, `_enterPathEdit`, is wired only into `CanvasToolMode.Select` — the black Arrow's double-click. So the white arrow can only edit a path the black arrow already opened, and used on its own it is inert.
  - That is the defect half of a longer report. The rest of it — hover previewing points and handles, clicking the line selecting the whole path the way the Arrow does, a modifier to widen the stroke — is an interaction model rather than a broken behaviour, and is in `ROADMAP.md` under Pillar 0 → *Vector selection that matches the hand it was learned with*. This entry closes when the tool can enter and pick on its own; it does not wait for the rest.
  - P3 rather than P2 despite the tool doing nothing at all: it is reachable through the Arrow's double-click, so no work is impossible, and vector editing is not yet every-session work. Cost: S for the entry as scoped.
  - **Fixed in the view model rather than in the canvas, which is what made it testable.** The first attempt branched inside `CanvasControl`'s pointer handler and worked; it was moved because the file's own division says the canvas turns events into coordinates and every decision about what they *mean* belongs to the view model — and because a gesture living in an event handler is one nothing can check. `GrabPathPart` now enters isolation when there is nothing isolated, then falls through to its own hit test, so one click both opens the line and takes hold of what was under it. Without the second half the first drag on a line is swallowed by the click that made it possible.
  - **A single click here against the Arrow's double, deliberately.** Q53's argument for the double-click is that reaching into geometry must never happen by accident — which is about the Arrow, where a click ordinarily means "pick this whole thing". It is the white arrow's only purpose, so there is no accident to protect against and a second click would be a tax.
  - The rest of the report — hover, click-selects-all, the widen modifier — remains the roadmap item. **Its first piece landed alongside this**: hovering a line now previews its points before any click, which is what makes the tool's own gesture discoverable.

- [x] **B134** `P3` `canvas` A stroke entirely outside the document cannot be picked, and will not be findable on an infinite canvas `evidence: AStrokeOutsideTheDocumentIsStillPickable, TheIndexKeepsUnclampedBoundsForPicking`
  - Found while writing `StrokePicker`, by moving a test line to y=700 in a 960×540 document and watching a marquee that plainly crossed it select nothing.
  - Cause is one clamp, and it is **correct where it lives**. `BrushEngine.SegmentBounds` clamps to `[0, info.Width] × [0, info.Height]` (`BrushEngine.cs:843-846`) because `CommitBounds` answers *"what do I repaint"*, and nothing off the surface needs repainting. `StrokeIndex.Of` builds from `CommitBounds`, and a stroke wholly outside gets `SKRectI.Empty` — indexed as reaching nothing, so `Intersecting` never yields it and the arrow cannot find it however close you click.
  - **The same distinction the picker already documents one level down**, arriving again: bounds and hit tests want different numbers. `StrokePicker` uses half the brush size rather than `DabReach` for exactly this reason, and this is that reason at the whole-stroke scale.
  - P3 because it is narrow today — a fixed-size document mostly keeps its art inside, and a stroke that merely *runs off* the edge still has clamped bounds that overlap the surface, so it is found. It stops being narrow the moment **infinite canvas** lands (`ROADMAP.md` has it `[~]`, with `TileStore`, `TileGrid` and `StrokeIndex` already built): art outside the frame is the point of that feature, and none of it would be selectable.
  - Fix: give the index unclamped bounds rather than teaching the picker to compensate. Either a `CommitBounds` overload that skips the clamp, or `StrokeIndex.Of` computing its own from `GeometryOps.BoundsOf` padded by dab reach. The clamped form must stay for the repaint callers, so this is an addition rather than a change. Cost: S.
  - Fixed as suggested: `BrushEngine.ReachBounds` is `CommitBounds` minus the clamp (same margins — dab reach, blur, clip feather — so pick-reach and paint-reach cannot drift), and `StrokeIndex.Of` builds from it, dropping its `SKImageInfo` parameter entirely so no future caller can reintroduce the paper. **What this deliberately does not fix:** `StampPaint` sizes its scratch with the clamped `SegmentBounds`, so ink still cannot *land* outside the document — a stroke drawn off-canvas is pickable, movable and deletable, but paints nothing until the paintable-extent surgery that true infinite drawing needs. That is feature work on the hottest file in the repository, not this entry's one clamp.

### colour

- [x] **B151** `P2` `colour` Dragging the colour wheel rescans and re-renders the whole document on every pointer event `evidence: AWheelDragScansTheDocumentOnceRatherThanOncePerEvent, TheNextRunLooksAgain, AStructuralEditDropsTheCachedFrameList, AnEventMidDragCostsAFractionOfTheFirstOne`
  - Reported as *"swatch recoloring. Canvas updates slow and delayed/stuttering"*.
  - `RepaintForSwatch` ran once per pointer event of the drag — its own comment said so — and each event walked **every layer of every cel of every open document**, ran `StrokesOf(frame).Any(...)` over the whole stroke record, invalidated every matching frame's cached raster, invalidated the compose ring, published synchronously (which then had to re-rasterize the current frame from its strokes, because the line above had just deleted it) and swept every thumbnail.
  - **Measured on a 120-stroke document: 62 ms of work per pointer event, and the first event and the twentieth cost the same.** That is the whole bug in one number — a wheel emits dozens of events a second, so the work arrives faster than it can be done and the canvas falls behind the hand. B30's replay cost is what dominates it (`n^1.00`, ~10.6 ms/stroke at the sizes B30 measured), so it gets worse in proportion to how much is drawn.
  - **Three fixes, and only one of them is "make it faster".**
    - **The scan belongs to the run, not the event.** Which frames a swatch reaches cannot change while the wheel is down — recolouring does not move a stroke's reference to it, and nothing can draw during the drag. Worked out once and kept until `CommitSwatchEdit`, which is the boundary every path that could invalidate it already goes through. `OnDocumentChanged` drops it too, for the structural edits that do not.
    - **A drag wants a repaint per displayed frame, not per event.** The first repaint of a run stays synchronous, so a one-shot recolour behaves exactly as it did; everything after it goes through `RequestSnapshot`, the coalescing post the shape tool's own live drag already uses.
    - **No thumbnail sweep mid-drag.** Nobody reads a thumbnail while the wheel is moving, and the sweep walks every cell of every row. The ids stay marked dirty and `CommitSwatchEdit` runs the one sweep at the end — owed *before* its early returns, because a drag out and back to the same colour still dirtied everything on the way past.
  - **After: 90.5 ms for the first event, 0.011 ms median for the twenty after it.** The second number is the *synchronous* cost only — the coalesced publish still happens, once per displayed frame, and that is the honest remaining cost rather than a saving. What went entirely is the per-event scan and the per-event thumbnail sweep.
  - **The guard is a count, not a stopwatch**, and that is the more interesting half. The property is not "this got faster", it is "this happens once per drag instead of once per pointer event" — exact, instant, and impossible to make flaky. `SwatchScans` is incremented in the scan and the test asserts it moves by one across thirty edits; it reads 30 on the old code. The timing test sits beside it because a count cannot see a scan that got slower, and it is a *ratio* so it means the same thing on any machine.
  - **What this exposes is bigger than the bug**, and it is worth stating where somebody will find it: `docs/DESIGN-performance.md` and the charter budget the *drawing* hot path, and the App suite's five performance tests are four about drawing plus one about the brush picker. Invariant 6 says painting is bounded work — and every budget we own points at painting. Every other gesture that runs at pointer rate into the same compositor (the wheel, the timeline scrub, the transform gizmo, the marquee, the node and handle drags, the segment pinch) has no budget at all. This one is the first of them to be measured, and its shape is the template. Cost: S

- [x] **B103** `P2` `colour` Undoing a project-palette recolour appears to do nothing `evidence: LivePaletteTests, UndoingARecolourOfAProjectPaletteRestoresIt`
  - Found alongside B102, and it predates Q30 rather than being caused by it.
  - `CommitSwatchEdit` records the undo step as `SetSwatchColor(d, id, colour)`, which walks `doc.Palettes` — the document's *own* palettes. A swatch belonging to a **project** palette is not in that list, so the delta finds nothing to change and the swatch keeps its new colour.
  - It looks like it works while editing, because the drag mutates the `Swatch` instance in place and the registry holds that same instance. Only undo reveals that the record and the object have parted company.
  - **Fixed.** `SetSwatchColor` now falls through to the project's palettes when the document's do not hold the swatch. The test uses a **project** palette deliberately: a document-palette test passed while this was broken and would have gone on passing, which is how the bug survived a suite that already covered live recolour.

- [x] **B102** `P2` `colour` A swatch edit repaints only the active tab, so other open documents keep the old colour `evidence: LivePaletteTests, RecolouringRepaintsEveryOpenDocumentThatUsesTheSwatch`
  - Found 2026-08-06 by checking rather than by a report, while answering whether palette updates sync across a scope. They sync **to disk** — the palette is shared and any file opened afterwards is correct — and they do not sync **across open tabs**.
  - `RepaintForSwatch` walks `Scene.Layers`, which is the active document's scene. Two of a character's animations open at once, recolour a swatch, and only the focused one repaints; the other keeps cached pixels until something else invalidates them.
  - Worse now that Q30 scopes palettes, because a shared palette is the thing a *set* of documents paint from — that is the whole feature — so the case where several of them are open is the ordinary one rather than the edge.
  - **Fixed.** `RepaintForSwatch` walks `Tabs` rather than the active `Scene`, so every open document holding a stroke that references the swatch is invalidated. The stroke-walk that decides *which* frames stays exactly as it was — a wheel drag does this per pointer event, and re-rendering frames whose pixels cannot have moved is what that walk exists to avoid.

- [x] **B22** `P2` `colour` A duplicated cel loses its link to the palette `evidence: ACloneKeepsItsLinkToThePalette, ADuplicatedCelStillPaintsFromTheSameSwatch`
  - Repro: paint with a palette swatch, duplicate the cel along the timeline (or generate an inbetween), then recolour the swatch. The original changes and the copy does not. Same for a gradient.
  - Cause: `Stroke.Clone` copied nine properties and not `SwatchId` or `GradientId`, so a cloned stroke fell back to its literal colour. It is what `DocumentEditor.CloneFrame` and the inbetweener both use, so it reached cel copy, cel duplication, drag-with-copy and every AI inbetween.
  - Fix: copy them. The list is exhaustive now and says so, because a field added to a stroke and missed here does not fail — it goes quiet. Found while writing break-link, which needed the same copy. Cost: S

### export

- [x] **B146** `P2` `export` Exporting a video appeared to do nothing, because every answer it had went to a hidden row `evidence: VideoExportSettingsTests, TheWindowSaysTheEncoderIsMissingRatherThanFailingSilently, AMissingEncoderIsASentenceThatSaysWhatToDoInstead, EveryReasonAnExportCannotStartIsSaidBeforeTheEncoderRuns, TheWindowOpensOnTheWholeTimelineAtTheDocumentsOwnSize, TheRangeFieldsCountFromOneTheWayTheTimelineDoes, ChoosingProResRenamesTheFileSoTheStreamMatchesTheContainer, ARangeAtHalfSizeEncodesToTheSizeItPromised, RenderingBiggerScalesTheSurfaceAndNeverTheGeometry`
  - Reported as *"saving a video now does nothing in the main menu. Not even feedback."* Repro: **File ▸ Export video…**, pick a filename, press Save — no progress, no file, no error, on a machine with no FFmpeg on PATH.
  - Cause is two things that look like one. `OnExportVideoClicked` reported **every** outcome — "Rendering video…", the finished filename, and `"FFmpeg was not found — …"` — by setting `MainViewModel.AiStatus`. That property is bound in exactly one place, a `TextBlock` inside the AI bar, and the AI bar is `IsVisible="{Binding AiEnabled}"`. With assistance switched off the row is **absent**, so the one surface that could explain the failure was not on screen. The export was working exactly as written and saying so into the void.
  - The second half is that there was nothing to adjust: the handler rendered the whole timeline at the document's size and the scene's rate, with the format inferred from the extension the artist happened to type. No range, no scale, no quality, no way to leave the sound out.
  - Fix: a real `VideoExportWindow`. The encoder's absence is said **first**, above every setting, with what to do about it and the PNG-sequence route that works today; the render runs while the window is open, the progress bar moves per frame, and the closing sentence names the file, its size and what was in it. Settings are a `VideoExportSettings` record — format, scale, frame range, rate, quality, sound — so the numbers are testable without a window on screen, which is where the previous version had nothing at all.
  - **The scale is a surface scale, never a geometry one** (invariant 7): `SequenceExporter.RenderFrame` takes it through to the compose canvas and the document is untouched, so a 200% render is the same mark larger rather than a re-seeded drawing. The declared size and the piped bytes are decided in one place, because a raw pipe that disagrees with `-s` by one pixel produces a green smear rather than an error.
  - **Two holes found by an adversarial pass over the first version of this fix, both worth writing down because both are the same defect wearing a different hat.**
    - **The size was rounded to even and that broke the sequence's promise.** Every codec here wants even sides, so `OutputSize` rounded — and at scale 1 a 101×99 document then encoded at 102×100 while `ExportPngSequence` rendered it at 101×99. `SequenceExporter`'s own class doc says a video and a sequence of the same document are the same pixels *by construction*, and this broke that at the window's own defaults, where nobody had asked for a resize. The encoder pads the odd edge instead, which is what it did before this window existed: **padding adds a pixel of background; rounding changes what was rendered.** The ProRes branch gained the pad filter the MP4 branch already had.
    - **The Sound box promised sound it could not deliver.** It was enabled from `Scene.Audio is { Muted: false }` — having a track, which is not the same question as being able to read it. A referenced WAV that has moved, or embedded bytes that will not decode, resolve to null, and the export then rendered a silent video and reported plain success. That is this very bug one feature along, which is why it is filed here rather than as its own entry. The window now asks the resolver at open, says *"The scratch track's file cannot be found"* on the box itself, and appends the reason to the finished sentence if the file goes missing between opening the window and pressing the button.
  - Cost: M

### layers

- [x] **B136** `P1` `layers` Every document opened from disk starts on the locked paper layer, so drawing does nothing `evidence: ADocumentOpenedFromDiskOpensOnAPaintableLayer`
  - Reported as "unable to draw on the last build — the cursor showed, no strokes appeared", and reproduced under Xvfb in one open-from-recents: the status strip says *Layer "Background" is locked — unlock it to draw on it* while every stroke bounces off the paper.
  - `NewDocument` has landed past the paper since the Background layer existed ("Land on something paintable"); `OpenDocumentTab` — the funnel for recents, the start screen and File ▸ Open — never set `State.LayerIndex`, so it defaulted to 0, which on any saved document with paper is the one layer that refuses strokes. `ReplaceDocument` had the same door (`ActiveLayerIndex = 0`).
  - P1 by reach: it is not an edge case, it is *opening your own work*. Masked for anyone who mostly created documents in-session and kept them open; found the day opening a saved file became the first thing the application asks you to do.
  - The regression test walks the whole route — open, draw, look in the record — because asserting the index alone would stay green if a second gate ever ate the stroke.

### project

- [x] **B163** `P1` `project` The project manager cannot be opened at all: clicking Project crashes the application `evidence: TheWindowOpens, EveryWindowsStaticInitialiserRuns`
  - Reported from a Windows build with two crash logs, five seconds apart — `ui-thread` then `unhandled`, which is one click producing a handled report and then the rethrow taking the process down. `0.1.0-alpha.12`, `runtime 10.0.10`.
  - The whole of it is one identifier:

    ```
    System.TypeInitializationException: The type initializer for
      'Lightbox.App.Views.ProjectWindow' threw an exception.
     ---> System.ArgumentException: Invalid application identifier (Parameter 'identifier')
       at Avalonia.Input.DataFormat.CreateApplicationFormat[T](String identifier)
       at Lightbox.App.Views.ProjectWindow..cctor()  ProjectWindow.axaml.cs:79
    ```

  - `CreateStringApplicationFormat` is the **cross-application** format, so Avalonia validates the identifier against what a platform clipboard will accept, and `"lightbox/status-card"` fails on the slash. Probed rather than guessed, against Avalonia 12.1.1: `-` and `.` are accepted, `/`, `_`, `:`, a space and the empty string are all rejected. Nothing in the message says which character, which is why the probe was worth two minutes.
  - **The card drag never needed a cross-application format.** The drop handler reads the `_draggedCard` field and never unpacks the payload; the comment beside the call already said *"the object is never leaving this window"* and *"the same call the project docker's row drag uses"* — and the docker's is `CreateInProcessFormat`, as are all six other drags in the application. So the fix is one word, and it makes the comment true: this was the only caller of the application variant anywhere in `src/`.
  - **P1 because a whole surface was unreachable**, not because of what it damaged. Nothing is corrupted and no work is lost — but Q41's window is the only place bulk status edits, tag edits and the export scope summary live, and the single click that opens it took the application down with it. It cannot be worked around.
  - **Why every test passed.** `ProjectWindowTests` drives `ProjectWindowViewModel` and deliberately never constructs a window — the split that makes 38 tests cheap. A static field initialiser is not reached by any of them, so the crash lived in the only part of the file the suite is structurally blind to. That is the shape `CLAUDE.md` points at when it says the hotspots list is full of XAML with no test beside it.
  - So the regression test is in two halves. `TheWindowOpens` constructs this window, which is the direct guard. `EveryWindowsStaticInitialiserRuns` runs the class constructor of all **15** windows in the assembly by reflection — it needs no UI thread and costs milliseconds, and it means the next window that ships a throwing static field is caught by a suite nobody had to remember to extend. Both were confirmed to fail against the unfixed line before the fix went back in.
  - Fix: `DataFormat.CreateInProcessFormat<string>("lightbox-status-card")`. The name loses the slash as well as the constructor, so moving back to an application format later cannot resurrect this. Cost: S
  - **Filed as B162 and renumbered after merge — which is the collision B148's entry predicted, arriving exactly as described.** B148 was renumbered *before* merge because somebody read the other open pull request's title and noticed; that entry then wrote down that this is "not a mechanism", and that "the next collision will not have a title to give it away." This is that collision. `fix/timeline/B162-dropped-frames-still-rendered` had the id in its branch name and its title, and it was still missed.
  - **Every check behaved correctly and none of them could have caught it**, which is worth being precise about rather than filing as a gap in the hook. `bugs.py ids` ran on push and was right: `main` did not yet contain the other B162, because PR #130 merged *between* this branch's push and its own. The two entries also landed in different sections — `project` and `timeline` — so git saw no conflict and both merges were clean. The collision came into existence only in the merged result, which is the case the file's header says CI is the last line for. CI then failed on `docs`, as designed, and that is how it was found.
  - So the mechanism B148 asked for is still unbuilt and is now twice-motivated: **`ids` comparing against the ids on every open branch, not only against `HEAD`'s parents.** Left unfiled as a bug again for the same reason — it is a change to the check rather than a defect in the code — but the cost is no longer hypothetical: two collisions, one caught by luck, one caught by CI after both had merged and after a third branch could have rebased onto it.

- [x] **B114** `P1` `project` A character's animations are invisible to export and to scoped resources `evidence: CharacterDocumentsAreInTheProject, AWholeProjectExportIncludesCharacterAnimations, AFolderPaletteReachesACharactersAnimation`
  - Measured, not reasoned about. A project with one character owning `walk`, plus one loose `background` filed in a folder that declares a palette:

    ```
    manifest.Documents            : background
    knight.Animations             : walk
    walk.FolderId                 : (null)
    whole-project export plan     : background          <- walk is missing
    palettes visible to walk      : 0
    palettes visible to loose doc : 1
    ```

  - **Two containers, and only one of them is wired up.** `ProjectIo.AddAnimation` puts the reference in `character.Animations` and never in `manifest.Documents`, and never sets `FolderId`. `ProjectIo.MoveDocument` treats the two lists as mutually exclusive — a document is *either* loose-and-foldered *or* a character's animation.
  - Everything built on the folder tree therefore reads half the project. `ExportPlan.DocumentsUnder` returns `manifest.Documents`, so **exporting a whole project silently omits every animation in it** — the main content of the thing the application is for. `ResourceScopes.Resolve` keys off `document.FolderId`, so a folder's palette, gradients, references, guides, templates and export preset **never reach a character's work**.
  - P1 on reach rather than on severity: it is silent, it affects the primary workflow, and it makes two shipped features (Q30 scoping, folder export) look implemented while covering the smaller half of a real project.
  - **`docs/DESIGN-project-scoping.md` is the write-up**: one container, folders scope everything, character-ness and scene-ness become folder attributes. Crucially the migration is **metadata-only** — no file moves, no path changes — because `DocumentRef.Path` already survives independently of the tree.
  - **The fix depends on a decision that is not this entry's to make** — whether a character stops being a container and becomes semantics attached to a folder, or whether the two lists stay and every reader learns to walk both. Raised as a question rather than guessed. The cheap interim, if the answer is slow: file character animations in the folder tree as well, so `FolderId` is set and they appear in `manifest.Documents`. Cost: M either way.

- [x] **B108** `P1` `project` Expanding a folder does not select it, so the reveal button and ＋ New both act on the project root `evidence: ProjectHierarchyTests, ExpandingAFolderMakesItWhereNewWorkLands, SelectingOnToggleDoesNotStopTheToggle, APressSelectsAnyKindOfRow, TheRevealButtonRunsTheSameCommandAsTheMenu`
  - Reported 2026-08-06 as two bugs, and they are one line: "show in file manager button does not open the selected file or folder — RMB show in file manager does work correctly, so perhaps the same path?" and "adding a new document while a folder is selected is not put in said folder but by default on the root (unassigned documents)".
  - **The reporter's own diagnosis was right, and it is the selection rather than the path.** Both surfaces read `Selected` as *where I am* — 🗁 reveals `SelectedPath`, ＋ New files into `TargetFolder` — and both fall back to the project root when it is null. Two gestures never set it: the **chevron**, which reaches into a folder and leaves the selection alone, and a **left press on any row that is not a document**, which the window's handler skipped. Right-click always set it, which is exactly why RMB worked.
  - Reproduced before fixing, with the chevron alone: expand a folder with nothing selected and `SelectedPath` is the project root while `TargetFolder` is null, so a new document lands at `unassigned-documents/walk.lightbox.json`. Both reports, verbatim, from one gesture.
  - **Latent since B86 and surfaced by B104.** With every row drawn flush left nobody used the chevron; with real indentation it is the first thing an artist reaches for. So making the tree legible is what exposed it — worth recording, because "the fix caused it" and "the fix made it reachable" want different responses.
  - **It refines B86 rather than reversing it.** That entry put the twisty on a Button because "collapsing and selecting are different intentions and one gesture cannot mean both", and that holds for the *intention*. What it missed is that a gesture which touches a folder must not leave the selection pointing somewhere else, because two toolbar surfaces silently read it as a destination.
  - The press rule now lives in the docker as `SelectFromPointer` rather than in the pointer handler, because synthetic pointer input is unreliable here and a rule that exists only inside a handler is a rule nothing can check. The ordering — select first, then ask which button and which kind of row — is asserted against the handler's source with comments stripped; the first draft of that assertion searched the raw text and was satisfied by its own explanatory comment, passing on a build with the bug still in it. Cost: S

- [x] **B106** `P1` `project` Moving a document leaves the original behind, so one drawing becomes two `evidence: ProjectHierarchyTests, MovingADocumentIntoAFolderMovesItsFileRatherThanCopyingIt, MovingADocumentBackToTheRootMovesItsFileToo, AMoveThatCannotReachDiskLeavesTheTreeAlone, ProjectDockerTests, MovingADocumentToAnotherCharacterMovesItsFileRatherThanCopyingIt`
  - Reported 2026-08-06 as it was met: "When moving root documents to a folder it seems to create a copy. Both the root document in the folder Documents and a copy of that file in the moved folder exist simultaneously."
  - **Cause, and it was deliberate.** `ProjectIo.MoveDocument` and `ProjectFolders.FileDocument` repathed the manifest and left the disk alone, on the reasoning — written in both doc comments — that "a move that deleted an artist's file because the tree was rearranged would be a poor trade for tidiness". The reasoning is right about deleting and wrong about the outcome: the next save wrote the document at its new path, so the old file stayed as an orphan the manifest no longer mentions. Nothing was lost and nothing was tidy, and the artist could not tell which of the two files the app would open.
  - A move is a **rename on disk**, which is what renaming a folder has always done through `MoveInProject` — nothing is deleted and nothing is left behind. The fix reuses it rather than adding a path.
  - **Ordering is the load-bearing part.** The disk moves first and the manifest only if that worked, because a manifest pointing at a path the file never reached is worse than either half: the panel would show the document in its new home and the drawing would not be there. `MoveInProject` refuses to write over an existing file, so a move can now fail for a reason the artist can act on, and the status line says so rather than the drag silently doing nothing.
  - P1 rather than P2: it is every drag in the panel, it duplicates artwork, and the duplicate is the kind that is only found later in a file manager. Cost: S

- [x] **B47** `P1` `project` Save as had no shortcut, and export and status never checked for a file `evidence: SaveRequirement, SaveGate, SaveFirstDialog, SaveFactsFor, SaveRequirementTests, SaveAndStatusGateTests, ShortcutRegistrationTests, AFileThatIsNoLongerThereIsNotTheSameAsNeverSaved, AStatusChangeAsksAboutItsOwnRowRatherThanTheActiveTab`
  - Repro, as reported: Ctrl+Shift+S does nothing. Then, separately: export and auto-export happily run on a drawing that was never saved, and a status can be set on one.
  - Cause of the first half, and it is duller than a broken handler: `Ctrl+S` was an `InputGesture` on the Save menu item and was never in `ShortcutMap`. So it could not be rebound, it did not appear in the shortcut editor — and because nothing enumerated it, nobody ever asked the next question, which is that **Save as had no gesture at all.** It was not a broken shortcut; it was never a shortcut. Found by writing the guard for the process rule rather than by looking for it.
  - Cause of the second half: nothing connected "is this on disk" to the two actions that depend on it. An export claims *this is what the drawing looks like*; a status claims *this is finished, go and use it*, and with auto-export on it immediately writes a sheet for an engine to collect. Both are claims about a file made to somebody else, and both were being made about files that did not exist.
  - Fix: one pure gate over three facts — is there a path, is there a file at it, are there edits the file lacks — shared by export and status so they cannot drift into disagreeing about what "saved" means. A status change is **prohibited** until there is a file, with two answers and no third: *Save file as…* or *Revert status change*. There is deliberately no "do it anyway", because that is offering a mistake.
  - **`SaveInPlaceFirst` is deliberately not blocking.** The artist already said where the file goes, so writing to it needs no permission and a dialog there would be a click between "mark Ready" and it being Ready.
  - **"The file was moved" is kept apart from "never saved"**, because the fix differs and so does the honest wording — writing back to a remembered path would put the file in a folder somebody deliberately emptied.
  - The gate asks about the **row being marked**, not the tab in front. Gating on the active tab would let one row through unsaved or block another for a reason not on screen.
  - Caught a real case immediately: `WorkspaceTests` asserted that clicking Ready on a freshly duplicated animation set the status, and a duplicate is explicitly not written yet — `DuplicateSelected` says "Save to write it to disk". The test was asserting the old behaviour; it now asserts both the refusal and, after a save, the success.

- [x] **B10** `P1` `project` Every swatch link dies when a project is saved and reloaded `evidence: VariantsRoundTripWithTheirPalettes, ASavedProjectKeepsItsSwatchIds`
  - Repro: make a project, paint with a palette swatch, save, reopen. The art no longer follows the palette; a variant resolves to no palette at all.
  - Cause: `ProjectIo` stored shared palettes as GIMP `.gpl`. That format carries names and RGB and **cannot carry ids** — `GimpPalette.Read` mints fresh ones — so every `Stroke.SwatchId` and every `Character.PaletteId` pointed at something that no longer existed.
  - Fix: store project palettes as JSON, ids intact. `.gpl` stays what it is — an interop format for the docker's Import/Export, not a storage format.
  - Mine, from the previous commit. Found by the variant tests rather than by review. Cost: S

- [x] **B187** `P2` `project` Autosave serializes the whole document on the UI thread, so the app hitches once a minute and the hitch grows with the painting `evidence: AutosaveServiceTests, TheWrittenFileHoldsTheStrokesAsTheyWereAtFlushTime, AFlushPersistsADocumentThatLoads, ASecondDirtyMarkAfterAFlushIsPersistedByTheNextFlush, FinishPendingWriteLeavesTheDiskQuietAndTheFileComplete`
  - Repro: paint a large document, leave autosave at its default minute, keep drawing. Every tick of the timer runs `DocJson.Save` — serializer plus disk — synchronously on the UI thread inside `Flush`, so the stall lands mid-stroke and scales with the document. B142 priced the serializer half of this at ~615 ms for a 5 000-stroke painting before its own fix; the disk write sat on top.
  - Found while working the file-size objective (this branch), fixed rather than filed per the ledger's own rule.
  - Fix: `Flush` snapshots with `Doc.Clone` on the calling thread — milliseconds at 5 000 strokes, B142's measurement — and serializes the clone to disk on a background task. The write races nothing because it owns a private copy; a tick that arrives mid-write keeps the dirty flag and retries later rather than stacking writers on one file. `PendingWrite` exposes the in-flight task so tests (and anything else that must observe the file) can wait instead of sleeping.
  - The race-guard test is the load-bearing one: a stroke added the instant `Flush` returns must not appear in the recovery copy — if it can, the writer is reading the live document while the artist paints on it.
  - **The hazard the move off-thread created, found by the adversary pass before merge:** with in-place autosave on, the background write and a manual Ctrl+S target the *same* path, and the fully synchronous old code was the only thing keeping them from interleaving. Unsynchronized they collide on the shared `.tmp` name — and the quieter, worse case is a stale snapshot finishing *after* a newer manual save and rolling the file backwards, which is silent data loss. The guard is `FinishPendingWrite`: every UI-thread save of a document file (`Save`, `SaveProject`) waits for the background write before touching the disk — a no-op when it is quiet, bounded by one write when it is not. `FinishPendingWriteLeavesTheDiskQuietAndTheFileComplete` pins the contract.
  - Cost: S

- [x] **B143** `P2` `project` Five derived getters write themselves into every saved document `evidence: DerivedPropertyTests, APinnedFrameDoesNotAlsoWriteAFlagSayingSo, AGuideDoesNotCarryTheAnglesDerivedFromItsKind, ADerivedPropertyOnTheDocumentModelIsMarkedAsOne`
  - Repro: serialize a document with a pinned frame, a guide, a reference strip and a gradient with an alpha track, then look at the JSON. `hasGhostFrames`, `angles`, `hasPivot`, `hasAlphaTrack` and `pivot` are all in it. None is read back — every one is a getter with no setter — so they are bytes written on every save that only ever travel outward.
  - **The same defect as `BlendOrNormal`, and the entry for that one is why this is a `P2` rather than a tidy-up.** `System.Text.Json` writes any public getter, so a convenience property added beside a nullable field reintroduces under a second name exactly the key that making the field nullable existed to remove. `Scene.GhostFrames` is nullable so that a document which pins nothing carries no machinery for pinning — and `hasGhostFrames: false` was written onto every document ever saved, which is that saving undone. `HasReferences` and `HasGuides`, six lines away in the same file, both carry `[JsonIgnore]` and both say in their remarks that they have to.
  - `Guide.Angles` is the worst of them: not a flag but an array, recomputed from `Kind` and `Angle` on load regardless, written once per guide. `ReferenceCell.Pivot` is the one worth remembering — a tuple's members are fields rather than properties, so it serialized as an empty `"pivot": {}` and looked like nothing.
  - Found by a test written for something else. `ImageResizeTests.EveryCoordinateOnTheSceneIsAccountedFor` enumerates `Scene`'s serialized properties to prove the resize visitor handles all of them, and `HasGhostFrames` turned up in a list it had no business being in.
  - Fixed: `[JsonIgnore]` on all five, and `ADerivedPropertyOnTheDocumentModelIsMarkedAsOne` sweeps every public getter-without-setter in `Lightbox.Core.Documents` so the sixth fails a test instead of shipping. Old documents still load — the keys were never read, so ignoring them on the way in was already the behaviour.

- [x] **B132** `P2` `project` A symbol cannot be placed on a vector layer, and nothing records a reason `evidence: ASymbolCanBePlacedOnAnyLayer, APlacementOnAFormerlyVectorLayerSurvivesAReload, AFrameWithNoPlacementsWritesNoPlacementsKey`
  - Repro was: add a vector layer, select a symbol, place it. Nothing happened. `MainViewModel.Symbols.cs:738` returned early on `activeLayer.Kind != LayerKind.Painted`, and the guard was silent — no status line, no refusal message, no cursor change.
  - Cause was structural rather than a policy: `PaintedFrame` had `Placements`, `VectorFrame` did not. The check existed because the field did not, and **no design doc, question or roadmap item ever gave a reason why a symbol should need raster pixels underneath it.** A placement is a reference plus a transform; it has nothing to do with a baseline PNG. `SymbolRasterizer.Render` already handled both frame kinds, so the renderer was written expecting this to work.
  - P2 rather than P3 on two counts. It was **silent** — the failure mode this ledger's conventions treat as worse than a crash, because the artist concludes the symbol is broken rather than the layer. And it **blocked Q52**: with the layer picker removed, every new layer would have been a stroke layer, so placing a symbol would have stopped working everywhere rather than on the layer kind nobody picked.
  - **Fixed by deleting the distinction rather than the guard, which was not the filed plan.** The entry proposed moving `Placements` onto both classes and dropping the check — correct, and it would have left two classes differing by exactly one field. Q52's named follow-on was taken instead: `PaintedFrame` and `VectorFrame` are now one `Frame` with a nullable baseline and nullable placements, so there is no kind left to test. The guard reads `if (activeLayer is null) return;` and the comment above it names this bug.
  - **The regression test sets `Layer.Kind` by hand, and has to.** There is no way left to *make* a Vector layer — that was the picker Q52 removed — so the only route to this bug is a document saved before the merge. Driving it through a command would test nothing; setting the field tests the file an artist already has on disc.
  - `Layer.Kind` survives as import provenance, so old documents need no migration and Q36 does not arise. What changed on disc is the frame: no `kind`, and no `pngBase64` unless there are imported pixels to hold. `PreMergeDocumentTests` pins that an old file still opens and still renders bit-identically, against a fixture and two fingerprints generated by the two-class build itself rather than by this one.

- [x] **B104** `P2` `project` The project docker draws no indentation, so the folder tree reads as a flat list `evidence: ProjectHierarchyTests, TheTreeIndentReachesTheRowTemplate`
  - Reported 2026-08-06 with three siblings, from the running app: "Docker: does not show indenting of the hierarchy."
  - **Cause: a view-model property nothing binds to.** `ProjectRow.Indent` has computed the right number since B86 — 14 px a level, one more for a document inside its folder — and the row template in `MainWindow.axaml` never referenced it. So `art/backgrounds/rooftops` drew three rows flush left, and the only thing distinguishing a folder from what is inside it was the glyph.
  - **Every existing test passed on the broken build, and that is the finding worth keeping.** `SubfoldersCanBeCreatedWithinFolders` asserts `[0, 14, 28]` and `ALooseDocumentGetsItsOwnRowWithNoCharacterAboveIt` asserts `0`; both read the view model, which was right. Nothing asserted that the number reached the screen. Same shape as the gizmo bindings in `BrushGizmoTests` — the value, the repaint and *the XAML actually binds it* are three separate claims, and the third is the one that had no test.
  - Fix: a left-docked spacer whose width is bound to `Indent`, rather than a margin on the row — the row's own background is the drag-and-drop target, and indenting it would shrink the area a drop can land on. Cost: S

- [x] **B99** `P2` `project` A new document is invisible: no badge, no docker row, no prompt on close, and a project save skips it `evidence: DirtyRevisionTests, ANeverSavedDocumentBadgesButOnlyPromptsOnceDrawnIn, UnsavedDocumentTests, ANewDocumentAppearsInTheProjectDocker, AProjectSaveWritesADocumentMadeWithFileNew, ClosingANeverSavedDocumentTakesItsRowWithIt, ClosingASavedDocumentKeepsItsRow, ClosingADocumentWhoseFileVanishedKeepsItsRow, ANewDocumentWithNoProjectOpenChangesNothing, SavingADocumentOutsideTheProjectTakesItOutOfTheProject, SavingADocumentIntoTheProjectRepathsItsRow`
  - Reported 2026-08-06 as two findings that are one. New documents come up *Untitled*, *Untitled (1)* with **no badge**; closing them prompts for nothing; `Ctrl+S` correctly opens Save as…, proving the app knows there is no file. And a new single file added under a project does not index into the docker.
  - **A document created while a project is open is in limbo.** No file on disk, no manifest entry, no docker row, `Source` null — and `SaveProject` clears only `if (tab.Source is not null)`, so it is neither saved nor reported. This is also **B79's third suspect**, arriving from the other side.
  - **It is a hole in B80, which is worse than the badge.** The close prompt collects `Tabs.Where(t => t.IsDirty)`, and a never-saved document is not dirty — so the one document with *nothing* on disk is the one the close handler will not mention. B80 shipped with this; the entry is here rather than reopened because the fix is the predicate, not the handler.
  - **Half landed, half did not, and saying so is the point.** The badge and the close prompt are built and guarded by `ANeverSavedDocumentBadgesButOnlyPromptsOnceDrawnIn`. **The docker-indexing half is not built**: a document created while a project is open still does not appear as a row. This entry stays open for that, because a bug half-fixed and ticked is worse than one left open — it is the thing that stops the ledger being trustworthy.
  - **The owner's decision, 2026-08-06: a new file badges, because it has not been saved at all.** Recorded with one nuance to settle when it is built — the badge and the close prompt want *different* predicates. Badge means "differs from disk", which a new empty document does. Prompting means "there is work to lose", which it does not, and File ▸ New followed by a close should not nag. B76's pending-versus-missing split is the same distinction: worth knowing is not worth acting on.
  - **The docker half landed 2026-08-06, to the owner's rule: a file created in a project gets a row marked unsaved, and closing it without saving removes it from the project view at once.** `File ▸ New` now files the document where the artist is — the same rule ＋ New ▸ Document follows — and `SaveProject` writes it, because the missing `Source` was what made a save skip it. `File ▸ New` still means *a new document* rather than *an animation under the selected character*: what changed is that the project knows about it.
  - **"Never written" is two conditions, and that is the part worth keeping.** Removal on close requires that no save has covered the id **and** that there is no file at its path. A document saved then edited is dirty and on disk, so closing it keeps its row; a document whose file was deleted from outside is not dirty, so it keeps its row and stays flagged missing — B61's rule that a vanished file is reported rather than removed on the artist's behalf. `ClosingASavedDocumentKeepsItsRow` and `ClosingADocumentWhoseFileVanishedKeepsItsRow` are the two guards, and they matter more than the fix: a close that quietly took a finished drawing out of the project would be worse than the bug.
  - **Adoption obliged a release, and the reporter caught its absence before it shipped.** Asked to verify "if I create a new document and place it outside of the project, it does not show in the project" — it did. Save As to a folder outside the project left the row in place, pointing at a path nothing had been written to and still saying *not saved yet*, and the next project save wrote a **second copy** inside the project. That is B106's one-drawing-two-files arriving from a different direction, and it is the general lesson: anything that takes a document into the project on creation owes it a way out when the artist chooses a home elsewhere.
  - Saved *into* the project is the opposite fix rather than the same one — the document stays and the **manifest follows the file**, path, name and folder, because a reference still pointing at the path it was created with also produced a duplicate, one directory over. `ProjectIo.RelativeInProject` is the counterpart to `ResolveInProject` that this needed, and it answers the only question that matters here: inside or outside.

- [x] **B94** `P2` `project` Dragging a document into the folder it is already in marks it unsaved `evidence: manual`
  - Reported 2026-08-06 from the running app. Nothing changed, so nothing should be marked.
  - **The no-op guard exists and is looking at the wrong axis.** `DropTargetFor` ends with `ReferenceEquals(destination, dragged.Character)` — it compares **characters**, which was the only way to group documents when it was written. B85/B86 added the folder tree beside it and the guard was never widened, so a drop that changes only `FolderId` reads as a real move even when the folder is unchanged.
  - Exactly the cost Q30 names: two hierarchies, and code that knows about one of them. The guard wants to compare the *destination scope* — folder and character together — and the fix is worth doing in a way that a third axis could not slip past again.
  - **Fixed.** `DropTargetFor` now compares folder *and* character, so a drop that would change neither is refused before it starts. `evidence: manual` because the drop path is a pointer gesture through Avalonia's drag service rather than a call a headless test can make — the same limit B75, B78 and B80 record.
  - The second half arrives free from **B98**: even a move that *is* real now marks the document only if it changed the document, because the badge reads the edit record rather than a flag someone set.

- [x] **B87** `P2` `project` No permanent delete option for files and folders `evidence: ProjectDockerTests, DeletedFilesCanBePermanentlyRemovedFromDisk, DeletedFoldersWithFilesPromptForConfirmation, EmptyFoldersAreDeletedWithoutPrompt, MissingFilesAreTrackedAndNotReloadedOnNextOpen`
  - Reported: the RMB context menu only offers "remove from project" which keeps files on disk. There is no option to permanently delete from both project and disk, and removed files should be marked so they are not unwantedly reloaded on subsequent checks.
  - Two operations are needed: remove from project (keeps file on disk, marks it as missing) and delete permanently (removes from project and disk). The permanent delete should show a confirmation prompt for folders containing files, but empty folders can be deleted without prompting.
  - Cost: M
  - **Fixed.** Two entries on the context menu, deliberately not one gesture with a modifier: **Remove from project** keeps the file, **Delete permanently…** does not. `ProjectIo.DeleteInProject` does the disk work and `ProjectViewModel.DeleteSelectedPermanently` the manifest work.
  - **The containment check is why that method exists.** Every path it is given comes from the manifest, which is plain JSON a person or an agent can edit, so `../../../Documents` is one careless entry away from an operation that deletes a directory tree. The full path is resolved and compared against the resolved root, and the comparison includes the separator — `Production.lbproj-old` starts with `Production.lbproj`, so a plain `StartsWith` would delete a sibling. Both guards were verified by removing the check and watching the two tests fail with a real bystander file on disk.
  - The confirmation is split so only the dialog is manual: `DeleteNeedsConfirmation` and `DeleteWarning` are properties the tests read, and the window only puts them on screen. The warning names the size of what is going — "Delete “Art” and the 1 folder and 1 document inside it?" — because *are you sure?* tells somebody nothing they can weigh. **Cancel is the default button**, which is the opposite of B75's save dialog and right for the same reason: Enter should land on the outcome that cannot destroy anything.
  - **Removing a folder returns its documents to the project root** rather than taking them with it. A drawing that vanished from every row because a folder was removed is a drawing that is gone as far as anyone can tell, and "remove" never touches disk.
  - **`MissingFilesAreTrackedAndNotReloadedOnNextOpen` needed no new machinery, and is worth having anyway.** The manifest *is* the index and nothing scans the folder, so a removed document already stays removed — the test pins a property that a later directory scan would silently break, and the folder tree makes such a scan tempting.
  - **One live crash found and fixed on the way.** `RemoveSelected` read `row.Character!` for anything that was not a document, so a scene row was a null reference — pre-existing, and made far easier to reach the moment folder rows existed. Folders are handled and the scene case reports rather than throws.

- [x] **B86** `P2` `project` Project docker is missing drag/drop, subfolder creation, and collapse/expand hierarchy `evidence: ProjectDockerTests, FoldersCanBeDraggedWithinProject, DocumentsCanBeDraggedWithinProject, SubfoldersCanBeCreatedWithinFolders, FoldersCanBeCollapsedAndExpanded`
  - Reported: within the project docker, the user expects to be able to drag and drop folders and documents to organize them, create subfolders within existing folders, and collapse/expand folders to manage the hierarchy.
  - Current behaviour is a flat list with no hierarchy support, limiting how artists can organize their projects.
  - Cost: M
  - **Fixed.** `ProjectFolders` in Core is the tree — Q29 answered that the docker and the project window are two surfaces onto one hierarchy, so it is model code and neither surface owns it. The docker renders it before characters and scenes, indented by depth, and a **＋ New ▸ Folder** entry creates one inside whatever is selected.
  - Collapse lives in the view model as a set of folder ids, not on the row. A re-read happens on every save (B61's watch), and collapse kept on rows would spring open each time the disk moved. That produced the one real bug found here: `Rebuild` reuses a row when it `Describes` the same thing and copies only Name/Status/Duration across, so a folder toggled shut stayed open on screen while the tree below it correctly vanished. `kept.IsCollapsed = fresh.IsCollapsed` is the fix, and it is the fourth field to need that line.
  - `MoveInto` is one entry point for the drop, because a tree offers one gesture and the model has two operations behind it — a folder reparents, a document is refiled and repathed. It returns false rather than throwing so a drop that cannot happen simply does not: dragging a folder onto its own child is an ordinary slip, not an error worth reporting.
  - **Still open under this id, deliberately:** dragging characters and scenes into folders. They are not in the tree yet — see **Q30**, parked by the owner. Cost: M

- [x] **B85** `P2` `project` Documents created in project subfolders are placed in top-level Documents folder instead `evidence: ProjectDockerTests, DocumentsCreatedInFoldersAppearInCorrectFolder, FolderStructureReflectsFileSystemHierarchy`
  - Reported: when creating a document through the project docker within a subfolder, it ignores the location and places the document in a top-level "Documents" folder instead.
  - This breaks the ability to organize documents by folder within a project.
  - Cost: M
  - **Fixed.** `ProjectViewModel.TargetFolder` is the selected folder, or the folder a selected *document* sits in — "new document" beside a document means beside it. `AddLooseDocument` files the new reference there through `ProjectFolders.FileDocument`, which sets `FolderId` and derives the path from the folder chain. With nothing selected the old behaviour stands and the document goes to `documents/`, which `WithNothingSelectedADocumentStillGoesToTheProjectRoot` guards — "put it where I am" must not become "put it wherever I last touched".
  - `FolderStructureReflectsFileSystemHierarchy` is the one that matters: every other assertion reads the manifest, and a manifest that is right while the save writes elsewhere is exactly what this bug was. It saves, checks the file exists at `episode-2/act-1/sc-014.lightbox.json`, reopens the project and finds the document in the same folder.

- [x] **B84** `P2` `project` Project docker creates a "project" folder in the wrong location on new project `evidence: ProjectCreationTests, NewProjectFolderStructureIsCorrect, AllFoldersAppearAtProjectRoot`
  - Reported: when creating a new project, a folder named "project" is created and appears within the Characters folder instead of at the project root.
  - Expected behaviour: all top-level folders created in the project folder should appear at the project root, not nested within other folders.
  - Cost: S

- [x] **B83** `P2` `project` New project is created with unwanted default subfolders `evidence: ProjectCreationTests, NewProjectHasCorrectDefaultStructure, NoUnwantedAssetFoldersCreated, AllDefaultFoldersAreListedInProjectFile`
  - Reported: when creating a new project, default subfolders are created that the user did not request — specifically **characters**, **shots**, **scene**, and **animation** folders. The palette folder with default swatches should exist, but work-related folders should only be created when explicitly requested by the user.
  - Additionally, every top folder created in the project folder should be included in Project.lbproj, and the project docker should show only system-required defaults, not asset folders the user must create themselves.
  - The root cause is that asset-organization folders are being created as defaults when they should be on-demand.
  - **Fixed, in two halves that read as one sentence but are different problems.** The first half was `NewProject` calling `AddCharacter(project, name)` — one line that invented a character named after the project and filed the artist's open drawing under it as an animation. Removing it leaves `documents/` and `palettes/`, and `NewProjectHasCorrectDefaultStructure` asserts that as an **exact set** rather than as a list of absences, because a list of things that must not appear passes for ever while a new one is added beside it.
